[ISSUE] guide /guide/cultural-warnings | title=Cultural Warnings - ChinaConnect
       EN: "Cultural differences and faux pas to avoid"
       EN: "The number 4 sounds like "death" (sì) in Chinese. Avoid giving gifts in sets of 4. Building floors and room numbers ofte"
       EN: "8 sounds like "prosperity" (fā). It is extremely lucky and often associated with wealth."
       EN: "6 sounds like "smooth" (shùn). It represents smoothness and good luck."
       EN: "White is associated with death and mourning. Avoid wearing white to celebrations or giving white gifts."
       EN: "Red represents luck, happiness, and prosperity. It is used in celebrations and given as gifts (red envelopes)."
       EN: "Wearing a green hat implies your partner is unfaithful. Never give a green hat as a gift."
       EN: "Knives symbolize cutting ties or severing relationships. If given, leave a small coin to "pay" for it."
       EN: "Clock sounds like "end" or "funeral" (songzhong). Giving a clock to Chinese hosts is very offensive."
       EN: "Umbrella is "Separation""
       EN: "Umbrella sounds like "separation" (san). Avoid giving as a gift unless you want to suggest parting."
       EN: "Pear sounds like "separation" (li). Do not give whole pears as gifts."
       EN: "Bring fruit (not pear) when visiting homes. Oranges and apples are safe choices."
       EN: "Do not stick chopsticks upright in rice (funeral ritual). Do not point with chopsticks."
       EN: "Turn slowly and wait if someone is reaching. Do not take food from someone elses section."
       EN: "When toasting, hold cup lower than elders or seniors. Empty cup means more drinking."
       EN: "Leaving a small amount of food shows the host provided more than enough."
       EN: "Personal Questions are Normal"
       EN: "Asking about salary, age, or marital status is not rude. It shows interest and care."
       EN: "A slight nod or bow is polite. Handshakes are also common in business settings."
       EN: "Business Card Exchange"
       EN: "Present and receive business cards with both hands. Take time to read the card before putting it away."
       EN: "Tipping is Not Expected"
       EN: "Tipping is not customary in China. Good service is expected as standard."
       EN: "Some Places Forbid Photos"
       EN: "Military areas, some museums, and certain attractions prohibit photography."
       EN: "Ask Before Photographing People"
       EN: "Always ask permission before photographing individuals, especially elderly or children."
       EN: "• Menu: Translation, GPS, Embassy, Culture"
       EN: "© 2026 ChinaConnect. All rights reserved."
